# Reviewed overlay sites: every place the C sources read a record through another type (LP64-005 Task 3).
#
# `python3 scripts/port/lp64_overlays.py list` prints the sites; `check` requires each to have exactly one
# line here and each line here to match a site. Format, one line a site, fields separated by tabs:
#
#   file:function:kind:text<TAB>VERDICT<TAB>reason
#
# The key is the site as `list` prints it: no line number, whitespace normalized, ` #N` on the Nth
# occurrence of one key in a file. A site whose text changes is a new site and needs a new verdict; its
# old line becomes stale and must be removed.
#
# Kinds (`lp64_overlays.py --help` has the exact patterns): index `((u16 *) x)[n]`; offset, a dereference
# of a pointer cast of a sum (constant or computed offset), of an address or of any other operand (an
# offset of zero), `*(T *) ((u8 *) x + K)`, `*((s32 *) x + 2)`, `*(s32 *) &p->member`, `*(s32 *) p`; opaque `x->padding[84]`; record `(ChrRecord *) p->voidp`;
# run, a Gfx, Model or record member followed by filler members. `list --locals` (stored scalar-pointer
# casts) is a survey aid and has no lines here.
#
# Verdicts:
#   typed       the object is of the type it is read as; for a run, every write and read through the head's
#               address uses the head's type and no more than its bytes (the reason names the writers).
#   same        the layout read is the same at both widths; the reason names the _Static_assert in
#               scripts/port/abi_probe/probe.c or probe_overlays.h that pins it, one that fails if the
#               layout drifts in the way that would make the site wrong.
#   overlay-64  deliberately different at 64 bits, behind IS_64_BIT; the reason names the assertion.
#   dead        not compiled or not reached on PC; the reason says why.
#   defect      the site reads or writes the wrong thing at 64 bits (or at both); the reason starts with
#               its owner, `Task N:` (the task that fixes it), `strict:` (a strict-tier file, fixed on the
#               strict branch) or `undecided:` (not decidable from the code), then what the site means to
#               reach and what it reaches at 32 and at 64 bits, measured with offsetof at both widths.
# `fixed` is not a verdict: a fixed site leaves the list, and its defect line becomes stale and goes with
# it. `check` prints the number of defect lines; `check --no-defects` fails while any remains.
#
# Lines are sorted by key (byte order, `LC_ALL=C sort`).
#
# Task 3's review batches add the lines; the last adds `check` to CI. Batch 1 (the index, opaque and run
# kinds), batch 2 (the offset kind) and batch 3 (the first half of the record kind: src/game/ bg.c to
# options.c) are in; the rest of the record kind follows.

src/game/bg.c:bgTestBulletHitBackground:index:((u8*)((Gfx*)point))[0]	defect	Task 4: means the command's opcode byte ([0], compared with G_SETTILE) and format bits ([1] >> 5) as the N64 stores them big-endian; on a little-endian host byte 0 of a Gfx is the low byte of w0 and byte 1 the next, at both widths (measured: w0 = 0xF5A00000 gives bytes 0, 0, ..., opcode at byte 3, i686 and aarch64). src/game/bondview2.c:mp_respawn_handler:record:(struct SetupIntroEmpty*)((intptr_t)intro_record+sizeof(struct SetupIntroEmpty))	defect	strict: the default case steps a WATCH (7) or CREDITS (8) intro record by sizeof(struct SetupIntroEmpty), 4 bytes, so the walk reads the record's following words as types; inherited from the N64 code, wrong at both widths for a watch record. Measured: SetupIntroWatch 12 bytes at both widths, SetupIntroCredits 8 and 16, SetupIntroCamera 40 and 56, SetupIntroEmpty 4. A watch record's hours or minutes word of 0 to 8 is parsed as that record type at both widths, and one of 6 (CAMERA) then steps 40 bytes at 32 bits and 56 at 64, so the walk diverges between widths; a credits record lands on its end at both widths (at 64 bits its pad word reads as SPAWN, 12 bytes, then the walk is at 16). Measured: the US ROM's 13 multiplayer setups hold 88 SPAWN, 13 ITEM and 13 CUFF intro records and no WATCH or CREDITS record (a scratch scan, counts only, not committed), so no stock multiplayer game reaches this case; the plan's play set is unaffected. Fix: step WATCH and CREDITS by their own sizes, as bondviewLoadSetupIntroSection does

src/game/bondview2.c:playerTick:offset:*((s32*)firingtable)	defect	strict: means the animation a firing table entry names, the ModelAnimation * in its leading union (struct weapon_firing_animation_table.anim, chr.h), which initResolveAnimGroupTable has resolved to an address; reads it through s32 *. Measured: anim at 0, size 4 at 32 bits (unk04 at 4) and size 8 at 64 bits (unk04 at 8), so at 64 bits the read is the low 32 bits of the address, sign-extended into intptr_t anim. The animations are in the memp arena, above 4 GB at 64 bits (LP64-003, portLp64CheckRegion), so the value is never the address: its upper half is all ones (bit 31 set, a non-canonical address) or zero (below 4 GB, where nothing is mapped). Reach: playerTick's block for each other player of a multiplayer game (getPlayerCount() >= 2, not the viewport's player, once g_PlayerTickCount reaches 2, the player alive) whenever its firing_animation_groups entry names a table and has anim 0: a remote player that holds a weapon held as a gun (groups 0 and 1, one- and two-handed) or a prop in each hand (group 3), every entry of which names a table, or one that is unarmed or holds an item not held as a gun (group 2), is not crouched and has a non-zero sideways speed, whether or not it also moves forward: crouch positions 1 and 0 take entry 5 first (bondview2.c:14722-14731), then bondview2.c:14732-14745 test only speedsideways (< 0 chooses entry 4, > 0 entry 3, each when its pointer is non-NULL), group 2's entries 3 and 4 being {&D_80030660[8], 0, ...} and {&D_80030660[9], 0, ...} (bondview.c:529-530); group 2's other entries (0, 1, 2 and 5) have pointer NULL and anim non-zero, so only a group 2 player that is crouched or has no sideways speed avoids the read. When the value differs from players_cur_animation (always the first time) and the body model has no anim2, it is passed as a ModelAnimation * to modelSetAnimation and saved in players_cur_animation; modelSetAnimation2 stores it in model->anim and calls modelSetAnimFrame, whose port guard portPtrLooksBad (port/src/model_audit.c) refuses only values below 0x10000 or misaligned, so modelConstrainOrWrapAnimFrame reads anim->unk04 through it: a fault on the first tick in which such a player is processed. Its match with cur also gates chrlvUpdateAimendbackShoulders. What a player holds at start and on respawn: bondview_r.c:225-245 sets starting_weapon[GUNRIGHT] (and [GUNLEFT] for a doubles record) from the first INTROTYPE_ITEM record of the setup's intro whose demo field matches check_ramrom_flags(), else ITEM_FIST (bondview_r.c:425-428); mp_respawn_handler (bondview2.c:12560) adds the same records' items to the inventory and bondview.c:1715-1716 equips starting_weapon; Spawn with Weapon (midas.ini [display] mp_spawn_weapon, default 0; portMpSpawnWeapon is a weak 0 in a headless build, port/src/libultra_shim.c) replaces the right hand's. Measured by a scratch reader of the US ROM's 13 multiplayer setups (Ump_setup*Z, 2026-11-18, not committed, deleted): each intro has exactly one INTROTYPE_ITEM record, right hand only, not for demos, naming ITEM_FIST, which has no WEAPONSTATBITFLAG_HOLD_AS_GUN, so group 2. So in a no-input run every player holds the fist and has no sideways speed: firingtable stays NULL, which is why the no-input multiplayer runs (all 13 arenas with 2 and 4 players for 1,800 frames, the stages set's mp- cases) do not fault, and they do not exercise this path. Task 8's respawn cases press START only, with Spawn with Weapon 0: the respawned player again holds the fist and no stick moves it, so they would not reach it either. The first run that does gives a remote player a gun-type weapon (picked up, or Spawn with Weapon on with a gun in the set's slot 1) or sideways movement while not crouched. src/game/gun.c:portGunTexAudit:offset:*(s16*)((u8*)id-8)	defect	Task 4: means the texture number of a model texture entry that texLoad loaded: its TextureID (uintptr_t, pointer-sized: probe.c) was replaced with osVirtualToPhysical(tex->data), and texLoad wrote the number as an s16 8 bytes (not a record's width) before tex->data, which holds at both widths. But the guard id >= MAX_TEXTURES also admits an inline texture entry, whose TextureID stays its segment-5 word 0x05xxxxxx after the load (the 64-bit converter writes 0x05000000 | offset, sub_GAME_7F075A90 promotes no texture entry, and portSwapInlineTextures finds such entries by (id >> 24) == 0x05), so for a model with an inline texture the read is 2 bytes at 0x05xxxxxx - 8, an address the game does not own, at both widths (for a served model; a 32-bit compiled-in model may hold the texels' address there instead, whose 8 bytes before are not a number either); at 64 bits nothing is mapped below 4 GB, so the read faults: not a layout difference, a guard that should also refuse a segment word (PORT_WORD_IS_HOST(id)). Stock models: a scratch scan of the US ROM's model files (2026-11-18, not committed) found inline texture entries in three prop models only, PgoldeneyelogoZ (2 of 2), PlegalpageZ (5 of 5) and PnintendologoZ (1 of 1), and none in the 92 gun or 80 character models; front.c loads those three with no texture pool (only GE_LOGOMODEL gives one, in the front end). So no stock load reaches the read; a replacement or added model with an inline texture entry would. Reached only by the GE_GUN_AUDIT diagnostic (pcsched.c runs portGunTexAudit once), for the six gun models it loads.

src/game/gunfire.c:gunUpdateAndFire:offset:*((s32*)nodeptr)	same	nodeptr is the read-write record of a shotgun's shell node (Switches[18 + i] or [23 + i]), set to whether the shell shows. modelGetNodeRwData returns the node's read-write record; the code tests no opcode, and these slots of the ROM model hold the node whose 4-byte flag the game sets (gun.c's sub_GAME_7F05E978 writes a gun model's Switches[10] to [13] the same way, as ->DisplayList.unk00). The verdict depends on which node the ROM puts in that slot, which the code does not establish: modelGetNodeRwData gives a read-write record for a header, display list, collision display list, Op07, LOD, switch, BSP, Op11, gunfire or head node, and the 4-byte write is that record's first word only for the switch, LOD, BSP, display list and Op07 records (a 4-byte int at both widths); the collision display list's record starts with Vertex *Vertices and the head placeholder's with a pointer (8 bytes at 64 bits), and the header's (s8 bytes), Op11's and gunfire's (s16) with smaller members, which a 4-byte write would overrun at both widths. Settled for the stock ROM by a scratch scan of the US ROM's model files (2026-11-18, not committed): every non-null slot these sites use holds a switch node (opcode 18): Switches[13] of 6 gun models, the 15 non-null Switches[18] to [27] of the shotgun models, and PcctvZ's Switches[3]; a replaced model could differ, and a check of the node's opcode at load would settle it for any model. Pinned: probe_overlays.h "a switch, LOD, BSP, display-list and Op07 read-write record starts with a 4-byte int"

src/game/objecthandler_2.c:load_object_fill_header:offset:*(s16*)((u8*)id-8)	defect	Task 4: means the texture number of a model texture entry that texLoad loaded: its TextureID (uintptr_t, pointer-sized: probe.c) was replaced with osVirtualToPhysical(tex->data), and texLoad wrote the number as an s16 8 bytes (not a record's width) before tex->data, which holds at both widths. But the guard id >= MAX_TEXTURES also admits an inline texture entry, whose TextureID stays its segment-5 word 0x05xxxxxx after the load (the 64-bit converter writes 0x05000000 | offset, sub_GAME_7F075A90 promotes no texture entry, and portSwapInlineTextures finds such entries by (id >> 24) == 0x05), so for a model with an inline texture the read is 2 bytes at 0x05xxxxxx - 8, an address the game does not own, at both widths (for a served model; a 32-bit compiled-in model may hold the texels' address there instead, whose 8 bytes before are not a number either); at 64 bits nothing is mapped below 4 GB, so the read faults: not a layout difference, a guard that should also refuse a segment word (PORT_WORD_IS_HOST(id)). Stock models: a scratch scan of the US ROM's model files (2026-11-18, not committed) found inline texture entries in three prop models only, PgoldeneyelogoZ (2 of 2), PlegalpageZ (5 of 5) and PnintendologoZ (1 of 1), and none in the 92 gun or 80 character models; front.c loads those three with no texture pool (only GE_LOGOMODEL gives one, in the front end). So no stock load reaches the read; a replacement or added model with an inline texture entry would. Reached for a load given a texture pool while portTickHwPace() is true: Hardware pacing on (Max FPS set to Hardware: midas.ini [display] maxfps=-1, or tickhz=hw; the default is maxfps=120, so off; the Classic preset sets it), in a stage, outside the watch menu, not in a demo or a recording. The loads with a pool in a stage: the first-person weapon and item loads, gun.c:976, 979, 986, 989 and 995 (&g_CurrentPlayer->item_related[hand], every weapon draw), and bondview2.c's multiplayer body, head and item loads (&pool). texnum then goes to portHwNoteTexture, the pacing model's cost.
